title:	No slot coordinator warnings using Lock Code Manager v3.2
state:	OPEN

### What happened?

When restarting Home Assistant, the logs contain the following for each of the managed code slots on each of the 4 locks.

2026-06-04 11:32:36.987 WARNING (MainThread) [custom_components.lock_code_manager.entity] 01KRH5ASYZM00844ST7FS5S15T (edh_locks): No slot coordinator for slot 7 when adding entity in_sync

2026-06-04 11:32:37.140 WARNING (MainThread) [custom_components.lock_code_manager.entity] 01KRH5ASYZM00844ST7FS5S15T (edh_locks): No slot coordinator for slot 7 when adding entity code

Other than the warnings, LCM seems to be working fine.   These warnings did not occur in the previous version of LCM and began appearing immediately after the upgrade.

### Steps to reproduce the issue

1.    Restart Home Assistant
2.    Observe logs
2.
3.
...


### Home Assistant Version

2026.5.3

### What version of Lock Code Manager are you running?

3.2

### Lock make and model

Schlage BE469ZP

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The warning is emitted from `entity.py:234-240` when an entity is added to Home Assistant but no `SlotEntityCoordinator` was registered for that slot in `runtime_data.slot_coordinators`. This is a race-condition or setup-order indicator — the entity was created before/without a matching coordinator.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me check how `slot_coordinators` gets populated:

```
591	def _find_shared_lock_instance(
592	    hass: HomeAssistant,
593	    config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
594	    lock_entity_id: str,
595	) -> BaseLock | None:
596	    """
597	    Return an existing BaseLock for ``lock_entity_id`` from another loaded entry.
598	
599	    A single physical lock may be referenced by multiple Lock Code Manager
600	    entries. We keep one BaseLock instance and share it: when a new entry
601	    references a lock that another loaded entry is already managing,
602	    reuse that entry's instance instead of creating a duplicate.
603	    """
604	    return next(
605	        (
606	            entry.runtime_data.locks[lock_entity_id]
607	            for entry in hass.config_entries.async_entries(
608	                DOMAIN, include_disabled=False, include_ignore=False
609	            )
610	            if entry.entry_id != config_entry.entry_id
611	            and entry.state is ConfigEntryState.LOADED
612	            and lock_entity_id in entry.runtime_data.locks
613	        ),
614	        None,
615	    )

639	async def async_unload_entry(
640	    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry
641	) -> bool:
642	    """Unload an entry, stopping tick managers before tearing down platforms."""
643	    hass_data = hass.data[DOMAIN]
644	    runtime_data = config_entry.runtime_data
645	    callbacks = runtime_data.callbacks
646	
647	    # Stop tick managers FIRST so no in-flight tick can keep calling
648	    # _perform_sync, coordinator.async_refresh, or _write_state once
649	    # downstream teardown begins. SlotSyncManager.async_stop is idempotent,
650	    # so the binary sensor's later async_will_remove_from_hass call into the
651	    # same manager is a cheap no-op.
652	    if runtime_data.sync_managers:
653	        _LOGGER.debug(
654	            "Unload: stopping %s sync manager(s)", len(runtime_data.sync_managers)
655	        )
656	        mgrs_to_stop = list(runtime_data.sync_managers)
657	        stop_results = await asyncio.gather(
658	            *(mgr.async_stop() for mgr in mgrs_to_stop),
659	            return_exceptions=True,
660	        )
661	        # Clear the registry explicitly so the lock-removed callbacks fired
662	        # below observe an empty set. Entity removal also discards each
663	        # manager during async_will_remove_from_hass, but that path only
664	        # runs if invoke_entity_removers_for_slot has populated slots --
665	        # which it may not when config has been migrated to options.
666	        runtime_data.sync_managers.clear()
667	        for mgr, result in zip(mgrs_to_stop, stop_results, strict=True):
668	            if isinstance(result, Exception) and not isinstance(
669	                result, asyncio.CancelledError
670	            ):
671	                _LOGGER.warning(
672	                    "%s: Sync manager stop raised during unload: %s",
673	                    mgr.log_prefix,
674	                    result,
675	                    exc_info=result,
676	                )
677	
678	    # Fire slot entity removal callbacks first so per-slot entities (which

682	    # at first setup, so ``config_entry.data`` is empty for any
683	    # normally-loaded entry.
684	    curr_slots = list(get_entry_config(config_entry).slots)
```

```
476	def _setup_entry_after_start(
477	    hass: HomeAssistant,
478	    config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
479	    event: Event | None = None,
480	) -> None:
481	    """
482	    Set up config entry.
483	
484	    Should only be run once Home Assistant has started. Update-listener
485	    registration is guarded by ``runtime_data.update_listener_registered`` so
486	    a reload racing with EVENT_HOMEASSISTANT_STARTED cannot stack multiple
487	    listeners on the same entry.
488	    """
489	    runtime_data = config_entry.runtime_data
490	    if not runtime_data.update_listener_registered:
491	        runtime_data.update_listener_registered = True
492	        unsub = config_entry.add_update_listener(async_update_listener)
493	
494	        @callback
495	        def _clear_listener_registered() -> None:
496	            runtime_data.update_listener_registered = False
497	            unsub()
498	
499	        config_entry.async_on_unload(_clear_listener_registered)
500	
501	    if config_entry.data:
502	        # Move data from data to options so update listener can work
503	        hass.config_entries.async_update_entry(
504	            config_entry, data={}, options={**config_entry.data}
505	        )
506	    else:
507	        hass.async_create_task(
508	            async_update_listener(hass, config_entry),
509	            f"Initial setup for entities for {config_entry.entry_id}",
510	        )
511
```

```
70	class LockCodeManagerActiveEntity(BaseLockCodeManagerEntity, BinarySensorEntity):
71	    """
72	    Active binary sensor entity for lock code manager.
73	
74	    Read-only view over ``SlotEntityCoordinator``. The coordinator owns
75	    the condition-entity subscription and the active-state computation;
76	    this entity only renders the current value.
77	    """
78	
79	    _attr_entity_category = EntityCategory.DIAGNOSTIC
80	
81	    @callback
82	    def _apply_coordinator_state(
83	        self, is_on: bool | None, inactive_because_of: list[str]
84	    ) -> None:
85	        """Apply coordinator-derived active state and write Home Assistant state."""
86	        self._attr_is_on = bool(is_on)
87	        if inactive_because_of:
88	            self._attr_extra_state_attributes["inactive_because_of"] = list(
89	                inactive_because_of
90	            )
91	        else:
92	            self._attr_extra_state_attributes.pop("inactive_because_of", None)
93	        if self.hass is not None and self.entity_id:
94	            self.async_write_ha_state()
95	
96	    async def async_added_to_hass(self) -> None:
97	        """Handle entity added to hass."""
98	        await BinarySensorEntity.async_added_to_hass(self)
99	        await BaseLockCodeManagerEntity.async_added_to_hass(self)
100	
101	    def _register_slot_coordinator_subscription(self) -> None:
102	        """Subscribe to the derived active-state view rather than the generic state poke."""
103	        # Type narrowing; the base only calls this hook when set.
104	        assert self._slot_coordinator is not None
105	        self.async_on_remove(
106	            self._slot_coordinator.register_active_view(self._apply_coordinator_state)
107	        )
108

124	        coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,

129	        BaseLockCodeManagerCodeSlotPerLockEntity.__init__(
130	            self, hass, ent_reg, config_entry, lock, slot_num, ATTR_IN_SYNC
131	        )
132	        CoordinatorEntity.__init__(self, coordinator)
133	
134	        self._attr_sync_status: str | None = None
135	
136	        @callback
137	        def _sync_and_write_state(in_sync: bool | None) -> None:
138	            """Sync _attr_is_on from manager and write HA state."""
139	            self._attr_is_on = in_sync
140	            self._attr_sync_status = self._sync_manager.sync_status
141	            self.async_write_ha_state()
142	
143	        self._sync_manager = SlotSyncManager(
144	            hass,
145	            ent_reg,
146	            config_entry,
147	            coordinator,
148	            lock,
149	            slot_num,
150	            state_writer=_sync_and_write_state,
151	        )
152	
153	    @property

155	        """Return whether binary sensor is available or not."""
156	        return BaseLockCodeManagerCodeSlotPerLockEntity._is_available(self) and (
157	            int(self.slot_num) in self.coordinator.data
158	        )
```

```
51	class LockCodeManagerCodeSlotSensorEntity(
52	    BaseLockCodeManagerCodeSlotPerLockEntity,
53	    SensorEntity,
54	    CoordinatorEntity[LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator],
55	):
56	    """Code slot sensor entity for lock code manager."""

60	    def __init__(
61	        self,
62	        hass: HomeAssistant,
63	        ent_reg: er.EntityRegistry,
64	        config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
65	        lock: BaseLock,
66	        coordinator: LockUsercodeUpdateCoordinator,
67	        slot_num: int,
68	    ) -> None:

70	        BaseLockCodeManagerCodeSlotPerLockEntity.__init__(
71	            self, hass, ent_reg, config_entry, lock, slot_num, ATTR_CODE
72	        )
73	        CoordinatorEntity.__init__(self, coordinator)
74	
75	    @property
76	    def native_value(self) -> str | None:
77	        """Return native value."""
78	        credential = self.coordinator.data.get(int(self.slot_num))
79	        if credential is None:
80	            return None
81	        if credential.is_empty:
82	            return ""
83	        if credential.is_readable:
84	            return credential.readable_pin
85	        # Unreadable code: fall back to the configured PIN so the sensor still
86	        # exposes the slot's intended value to consumers like the sync layer.
87	        return self.coordinator.desired_credential(int(self.slot_num)).readable_pin
88	
89	    @property
90	    def available(self) -> bool:
91	        """Return whether sensor is available or not."""
92	        return BaseLockCodeManagerCodeSlotPerLockEntity._is_available(self) and (
93	            int(self.slot_num) in self.coordinator.data
94	        )

97	        """Handle entity added to hass."""
98	        await BaseLockCodeManagerCodeSlotPerLockEntity.async_added_to_hass(self)
99	        await CoordinatorEntity.async_added_to_hass(self)
100	
101	        if self.native_value is None:
102	            self.hass.async_create_task(
103	                self.async_update(), f"Force update {self.entity_id}"
104	            )
```

```
757	async def _async_setup_new_locks(
758	    hass: HomeAssistant,
759	    config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
760	    locks_to_add: Sequence[str],
761	    new_config: EntryConfig,
762	    callbacks: Any,
763	    ent_reg: er.EntityRegistry,
764	) -> None:
765	    """Set up newly added locks and create per-slot entities for them."""
766	    entry_id = config_entry.entry_id
767	    entry_title = config_entry.title
768	    runtime_data = config_entry.runtime_data
769	
770	    _LOGGER.debug(
771	        "%s (%s): Adding following locks: %s",
772	        entry_id,
773	        entry_title,
774	        locks_to_add,
775	    )
776	
777	    async def _setup_one_lock(lock_entity_id: str) -> BaseLock:
778	        existing_lock = _find_shared_lock_instance(hass, config_entry, lock_entity_id)
779	        if existing_lock is not None:
780	            _LOGGER.debug(
781	                "%s (%s): Reusing lock instance for lock %s",
782	                entry_id,
783	                entry_title,
784	                existing_lock,
785	            )
786	            runtime_data.locks[lock_entity_id] = existing_lock
787	            await existing_lock.async_wait_for_setup()
788	            return existing_lock
789	
790	        lock = runtime_data.locks[lock_entity_id] = async_create_lock_instance(
791	            hass,
792	            dr.async_get(hass),
793	            ent_reg,
794	            config_entry,
795	            lock_entity_id,
796	        )
797	        _LOGGER.debug(
798	            "%s (%s): Creating lock instance for lock %s",
799	            entry_id,
800	            entry_title,
801	            lock,
802	        )
803	        await lock.async_setup_internal(config_entry)
804	        return lock
805	
806	    # Set up locks concurrently. Each lock's initial usercode fetch can take
807	    # seconds (Z-Wave node poll, Schlage HTTP, Matter device read); serial
808	    # setup meant lock N+1 only began once lock N finished. return_exceptions
809	    # isolates per-lock failures so one bad lock does not block the others.
810	    setup_results = await asyncio.gather(
811	        *(_setup_one_lock(lock_entity_id) for lock_entity_id in locks_to_add),
812	        return_exceptions=True,
813	    )
814	
815	    added_locks: list[BaseLock] = []
816	    for lock_entity_id, result in zip(locks_to_add, setup_results, strict=True):
817	        if isinstance(result, BaseException):
818	            _LOGGER.error(
819	                "%s (%s): Failed to set up lock %s: %s",
820	                entry_id,
821	                entry_title,
822	                lock_entity_id,
823	                result,
824	                exc_info=result,
825	            )
826	            runtime_data.locks.pop(lock_entity_id, None)
827	            continue
828	
829	        added_locks.append(result)
830	
831	        if not await result.async_internal_is_integration_connected():
832	            _LOGGER.debug(
833	                "%s (%s): Lock %s is not connected yet. Entities will be created "

855	async def async_update_listener(
856	    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry
857	) -> None:
858	    """Update listener."""
859	    # Refresh the cached EntryConfig on EVERY update — including entity-driven
860	    # writes that go straight to data with empty options (e.g. a slot's name or
861	    # PIN being edited via its text entity). The early-return below skips the
862	    # entity-creation pass for those cases, but downstream readers via
863	    # runtime_data.config still need to see the current data.
864	    runtime_data = config_entry.runtime_data
865	    runtime_data.config = EntryConfig.from_entry(config_entry)
866	
867	    # Notify per-slot coordinators so derived "active" state and condition-
868	    # entity subscriptions stay in sync with the refreshed config view.
869	    # Runs on both the entity-driven path (early return below) and the
870	    # options-flow path so a calendar/condition swap is picked up the
871	    # same way.
```

```
855	async def async_update_listener(
856	    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry
857	) -> None:
858	    """Update listener."""
859	    # Refresh the cached EntryConfig on EVERY update — including entity-driven
860	    # writes that go straight to data with empty options (e.g. a slot's name or
861	    # PIN being edited via its text entity). The early-return below skips the
862	    # entity-creation pass for those cases, but downstream readers via
863	    # runtime_data.config still need to see the current data.
864	    runtime_data = config_entry.runtime_data
865	    runtime_data.config = EntryConfig.from_entry(config_entry)
866	
867	    # Notify per-slot coordinators so derived "active" state and condition-
868	    # entity subscriptions stay in sync with the refreshed config view.
869	    # Runs on both the entity-driven path (early return below) and the
870	    # options-flow path so a calendar/condition swap is picked up the
871	    # same way.
872	    for coordinator in runtime_data.slot_coordinators.values():
873	        coordinator.notify_config_changed()
874	
875	    # No need to do entity creation/removal work if there are no options
876	    # because that only happens at the end of this function (data + empty
877	    # options = the post-listener state we just wrote ourselves).
878	    if not config_entry.options:
879	        return
880	
881	    ent_reg = er.async_get(hass)
882	
883	    entry_id = config_entry.entry_id

886	
887	    setup_tasks = runtime_data.setup_tasks
888	
889	    # Build EntryConfig views of data (old) and options (new) so all
890	    # downstream slot lookups use int keys regardless of how the storage
891	    # round-tripped them. Callers that need a plain dict (e.g. to hand
892	    # back to async_update_entry at the end of this function) call
893	    # to_dict() at the write site.
894	    old_config = EntryConfig.from_mapping(config_entry.data)
895	    new_config = EntryConfig.from_mapping(config_entry.options)
896	    new_slots = new_config.slots
897	
898	    # Set up any platforms that the new slot configs need that haven't
899	    # already been set up. The number_of_uses deprecation cleanup runs
900	    # in async_setup_entry before platform forwarding, not here.
901	    for platform in {
902	        platform
903	        for slot_config in new_slots.values()
904	        for key, platform in PLATFORM_MAP.items()
905	        if key in slot_config
906	        and platform not in setup_tasks
907	        and platform != Platform.CALENDAR
908	    }:
909	        setup_tasks[platform] = config_entry.async_create_task(
910	            hass,
911	            hass.config_entries.async_forward_entry_setups(config_entry, [platform]),
912	            "setup_new_platforms",
913	        )
914	    await asyncio.gather(*setup_tasks.values())
915

919	    locks_to_add = diff.locks_added
920	    locks_to_remove = diff.locks_removed
921	
922	    callbacks = runtime_data.callbacks

924	    # Remove slot entities first so per-slot entities (which reference locks)
```

```
1	"""
2	Per-slot entity coordinator.
3	
4	A SlotEntityCoordinator instance owns the per-slot state surface that
5	text, switch, and active-binary-sensor entities used to compute on their
6	own. Entities become read-only views over the coordinator: they register
7	write callbacks for state changes and dispatch user intent (set a PIN,
8	toggle enabled) through the coordinator. The coordinator updates the
9	canonical config entry, manages slot-level repair issues, and asks the
10	per-lock SlotSyncManagers to re-evaluate on the next tick.
11	
12	There is one SlotEntityCoordinator per (config_entry, slot_num); the per-
13	lock SlotSyncManager remains one per (config_entry, slot_num, lock).
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	from collections.abc import Callable
19	import logging
20	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
21	
22	from homeassistant.const import (
23	    CONF_ENABLED,
24	    CONF_ENTITY_ID,
25	    CONF_NAME,
26	    CONF_PIN,
27	    STATE_OFF,
28	    STATE_ON,
29	)
30	from homeassistant.core import (
31	    Event,
32	    EventStateChangedData,
33	    HomeAssistant,
34	    callback,
35	)
36	from homeassistant.helpers.event import async_track_state_change_event
37	from homeassistant.helpers.issue_registry import (
38	    IssueSeverity,
39	    async_create_issue,
40	    async_delete_issue,
41	)
42	
43	from ..const import ATTR_IN_SYNC, DOMAIN, EVENT_PIN_USED
44	from .config import EntryConfig
45	from .queries import get_entry_config
46	
47	if TYPE_CHECKING:
48	    from .models import LockCodeManagerConfigEntry
49	    from .sync import SlotSyncManager
50	
51	
52	_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
53	
54	
55	ActiveViewWriter = Callable[[bool | None, list[str]], None]
56	
57	
58	class SlotEntityCoordinator:
59	    """
60	    Coordinate per-slot entity state for one (config entry, slot) pair.
61	
62	    Computes the "active" derived state from the slot config plus the
63	    optional condition entity, fans out updates to the active binary
64	    sensor, owns slot-level repair issues (``pin_required``), and provides
65	    a single intent-dispatch surface so text and switch entities do not
66	    have to mutate the config entry or call sibling-entity services
67	    directly.
68	
69	    The coordinator does not own the per-lock SlotSyncManager state
70	    machine; it just keeps a reference to the managers for this slot so
71	    it can request a sync check after a user-visible state change.
72	    """
73	
74	    def __init__(
75	        self,
76	        hass: HomeAssistant,
77	        config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry,
78	        slot_num: int,
79	    ) -> None:
80	        """Initialize the coordinator."""
81	        self._hass = hass
82	        self._config_entry = config_entry
83	        self._slot_num = int(slot_num)
84	        self._log_prefix = (
85	            f"{config_entry.entry_id} ({config_entry.title}): slot {slot_num}"
86	        )
87	
88	        self._active_view_writers: set[ActiveViewWriter] = set()
89	        self._state_subscribers: set[Callable[[], None]] = set()
90	        self._sync_managers: set[SlotSyncManager] = set()
```

```
+    # setup meant lock N+1 only began once lock N finished. return_exceptions
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The PR #1191 introduced `SlotEntityCoordinator` and a comment that says "Create the per-slot coordinator first so entities can look it up in their constructor / async_added_to_hass." But the author only placed coordinator creation inside the `slots_to_add` loop — they missed that `_async_setup_new_locks` runs BEFORE that loop and adds per-lock entities for those same new slots. So during fresh setup (slots ARE new AND locks ARE new at the same time), per-lock entities for new locks at new slots get scheduled before their coordinator exists.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me confirm `SlotEntityCoordinator.__init__` and `async_start()` don't depend on locks already being set up:

> AGENT

I've diagnosed the bug. Here's what I found:

## Diagnosis

The warnings come from `entity.py:234` — emitted when an entity's `async_added_to_hass` runs but `runtime_data.slot_coordinators` has no entry for its slot.

**Root cause** (introduced in PR #1191 "make text/switch/active sensor read-only views", May 30):

In `async_update_listener` (`__init__.py:967-984`), the order is:

1. **`_async_setup_new_locks(...)`** runs first — this schedules per-lock entities (`code` sensor, `in_sync` binary sensor) for new locks at every slot via `invoke_lock_slot_adders`.
2. **Then** the `for slot_num in slots_to_add:` loop creates the `SlotEntityCoordinator` for each new slot.

`_async_setup_new_locks` contains `await` points (one per lock for `async_internal_is_integration_connected`), giving the event loop a chance to drain scheduled entity-add tasks. So per-lock entities for lock-1 can call `async_added_to_hass` while lock-2's setup is still awaiting — and at that moment, the slot coordinators haven't been created yet.

PR #1191's own comment said *"Create the per-slot coordinator first so entities can look it up"* — but only put it before `invoke_standard_adders`, missing that `_async_setup_new_locks` adds per-lock entities for new slots even earlier. This wasn't a problem before that PR because there was no coordinator dependency.

The user is hitting this exactly at startup: all 4 locks AND all slots are "new" from the diff's perspective (config gets migrated `data → options` on first start), so both `_async_setup_new_locks` and the slot loop fire on the same path.

**Fix:** hoist coordinator creation in front of `_async_setup_new_locks`. The coordinator's `__init__`/`async_start()` are lock-independent (only subscribe to condition entity + compute derived active state).

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This is a classic ordering bug where a coupling (per-lock entities now depend on per-slot coordinators) was added without auditing every entity-creation path. The diff path has two entity-creation entry points — the new-locks path and the new-slots path — and only the second was fixed up to create coordinators first.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The fix splits the `slots_to_add` loop in two: a tight upfront pass that just creates coordinators (no awaits), then `_async_setup_new_locks`, then the original loop minus the coordinator-creation lines. This preserves the existing behavior order for everything else (standard adders, per-lock adders for non-new locks) — we only change *when* the coordinator dict gets populated.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

The mock lock's `async_is_integration_connected` returns synchronously (no actual yield), so the event loop never drains scheduled entity-add tasks during `_async_setup_new_locks`. The bug only triggers when there's a real yield (real Z-Wave JS). Let me force the yield to reliably reproduce:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01TzhefQurxjwXcPE3DPGdE7
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/test_init.py",
  "old_string": "async def test_no_slot_coordinator_warning_during_initial_setup(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    mock_lock_config_entry,\n    lock_code_manager_config_entry,\n    caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Regression test for issue #1213.\n\n    Before the fix, per-lock entities (``code`` sensor, ``in_sync`` binary\n    sensor) were scheduled by ``_async_setup_new_locks`` while the slot\n    coordinators had not yet been created. The await on\n    ``async_internal_is_integration_connected`` between locks let the event\n    loop drain the entity-add tasks for prior locks, whose\n    ``async_added_to_hass`` then warned about the missing coordinator.\n    \"\"\"\n    assert \"No slot coordinator\" not in caplog.text",
  "new_string": "@pytest.mark.parametrize(\"config\", [{}])\nasync def test_no_slot_coordinator_warning_during_initial_setup(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    mock_lock_config_entry,\n    caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Regression test for issue #1213.\n\n    Before the fix, per-lock entities (``code`` sensor, ``in_sync`` binary\n    sensor) were scheduled by ``_async_setup_new_locks`` while the slot\n    coordinators had not yet been created. The await on\n    ``async_internal_is_integration_connected`` between locks let the event\n    loop drain the entity-add tasks for prior locks, whose\n    ``async_added_to_hass`` then warned about the missing coordinator.\n\n    The race requires the connection check to actually yield to the event\n    loop -- the default mock returns synchronously and so cannot reproduce\n    it. We patch it to force an ``asyncio.sleep(0)`` yield, which is what\n    real provider calls (Z-Wave JS, Matter) do.\n    \"\"\"\n    from .common import MockLCMLock\n\n    async def _yielding_is_connected(self: MockLCMLock) -> bool:\n        await asyncio.sleep(0)\n        return self._connected\n\n    config_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN, data=BASE_CONFIG, unique_id=\"Issue 1213\"\n    )\n    config_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    caplog.set_level(logging.WARNING)\n    with patch.object(\n        MockLCMLock, \"async_is_integration_connected\", _yielding_is_connected\n    ):\n        assert await hass.config_entries.async_setup(config_entry.entry_id)\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n    assert \"No slot coordinator\" not in caplog.text\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(config_entry.entry_id)"
}
```

```
--- a/custom_components/lock_code_manager/__init__.py
+++ b/custom_components/lock_code_manager/__init__.py
@@ -964,6 +964,17 @@ async def async_update_listener(
             hass, config_entry, lock_entity_id=lock_entity_id, remove_permanently=True
         )
 
+    # Create per-slot coordinators for new slots BEFORE setting up new
+    # locks. _async_setup_new_locks awaits per-lock connection checks,
+    # giving the event loop opportunities to drain entity-add tasks it
+    # scheduled for prior locks; those per-lock entities (`code`,
+    # `in_sync`) look up the slot coordinator in async_added_to_hass and
+    # would warn if it did not yet exist.
+    for slot_num in slots_to_add:
+        coordinator = SlotEntityCoordinator(hass, config_entry, slot_num)
+        runtime_data.slot_coordinators[slot_num] = coordinator
+        coordinator.async_start()
+
     if locks_to_add:
         await _async_setup_new_locks(
             hass, config_entry, locks_to_add, new_config, callbacks, ent_reg
@@ -978,9 +989,6 @@ async def async_update_listener(
             entry_title,
             slot_num,
         )
-        coordinator = SlotEntityCoordinator(hass, config_entry, slot_num)
-        runtime_data.slot_coordinators[slot_num] = coordinator
-        coordinator.async_start()
         callbacks.invoke_standard_adders(slot_num, ent_reg)
 
         for lock_entity_id, lock in runtime_data.locks.items():
diff --git a/tests/test_init.py b/tests/test_init.py
index a35167be..4f8565a1 100644
--- a/tests/test_init.py
+++ b/tests/test_init.py
@@ -53,6 +53,7 @@ from .common import (
     LOCK_1_ENTITY_ID,
     LOCK_2_ENTITY_ID,
     SLOT_1_IN_SYNC_ENTITY,
+    MockLCMLock,
 )
 from .conftest import (
     async_initial_tick,
@@ -641,6 +642,46 @@ async def test_coordinator_exists_after_setup(
         assert lock.coordinator is not None
 
 
+async def test_no_slot_coordinator_warning_during_initial_setup(
+    hass: HomeAssistant,
+    mock_lock_config_entry,
+    caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,
+) -> None:
+    """Regression test for issue #1213.
+
+    Before the fix, per-lock entities (``code`` sensor, ``in_sync`` binary
+    sensor) were scheduled by ``_async_setup_new_locks`` while the slot
+    coordinators had not yet been created. The await on
+    ``async_internal_is_integration_connected`` between locks let the event
+    loop drain the entity-add tasks for prior locks, whose
+    ``async_added_to_hass`` then warned about the missing coordinator.
+
+    The race requires the connection check to actually yield to the event
+    loop -- the default mock returns synchronously and so cannot reproduce
+    it. We patch it to force an ``asyncio.sleep(0)`` yield, which is what
+    real provider calls (Z-Wave JS, Matter) do.
+    """
+
+    async def _yielding_is_connected(self: MockLCMLock) -> bool:
+        await asyncio.sleep(0)
+        return self._connected
+
+    config_entry = MockConfigEntry(
+        domain=DOMAIN, data=BASE_CONFIG, unique_id="Issue 1213"
+    )
+    config_entry.add_to_hass(hass)
+    caplog.set_level(logging.WARNING)
+    with patch.object(
+        MockLCMLock, "async_is_integration_connected", _yielding_is_connected
+    ):
+        assert await hass.config_entries.async_setup(config_entry.entry_id)
+        await hass.async_block_till_done()
+
+    assert "No slot coordinator" not in caplog.text
+
+    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(config_entry.entry_id)
+
+
 @pytest.mark.parametrize("config", [{}])
 async def test_lovelace_updated_on_structural_change(
     hass: HomeAssistant,
```

```json
{
  "command": "git add -A && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix: create per-slot coordinators before per-lock entity setup\n\nCloses #1213.\n\nPR #1191 introduced ``SlotEntityCoordinator`` and a requirement that\nentities look it up in ``async_added_to_hass``. The slot loop in\n``async_update_listener`` already created the coordinator before the\nstandard slot entities were added, but ``_async_setup_new_locks`` runs\nearlier on the same path and schedules per-lock ``code`` and\n``in_sync`` entities for every slot. Its per-lock await on\n``async_internal_is_integration_connected`` lets the event loop drain\nthose scheduled entity-add tasks before control returns and the slot\nloop creates the coordinators, so per-lock entities for the new locks\nemit the \"No slot coordinator for slot N\" warning during startup.\n\nHoist coordinator creation for ``slots_to_add`` ahead of the\n``_async_setup_new_locks`` call so per-lock entities scheduled there\ncan resolve their coordinator. ``SlotEntityCoordinator.__init__`` and\n``async_start`` are lock-independent (condition-entity subscription\nplus derived active-state recompute), so creating them first is safe.\n\nThe default ``MockLCMLock.async_is_integration_connected`` returns\nwithout yielding, so unit tests cannot reproduce the race. The\nregression test patches it to ``asyncio.sleep(0)`` to mimic real\nprovider behavior (Z-Wave JS, Matter) and asserts no warning is logged.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit the fix and test"
}
```

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"fix: create per-slot coordinators before per-lock entity setup\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nPR #1191 introduced `SlotEntityCoordinator` and a requirement that entities resolve it from `runtime_data.slot_coordinators` in `async_added_to_hass`. The `slots_to_add` loop in `async_update_listener` created the coordinator before invoking standard slot adders, but `_async_setup_new_locks` runs **earlier on the same path** and schedules per-lock `code` (sensor) and `in_sync` (binary sensor) entities for every slot. Its per-lock `await result.async_internal_is_integration_connected()` gives the event loop a chance to drain the entity-add tasks scheduled for already-processed locks before control returns to the slot loop that creates the coordinators. The per-lock entities' `async_added_to_hass` then runs with `runtime_data.slot_coordinators` still empty and emits:\n\n```\nWARNING ... No slot coordinator for slot N when adding entity in_sync\nWARNING ... No slot coordinator for slot N when adding entity code\n```\n\nThis is exactly what @AA6CG hit at startup with 4 locks: on first load the diff sees all locks and all slots as \"added,\" so both paths fire on the same call.\n\nThis PR hoists coordinator creation for `slots_to_add` ahead of `_async_setup_new_locks` so per-lock entities scheduled there can resolve their coordinator. `SlotEntityCoordinator.__init__` / `async_start` are lock-independent (they only subscribe to the condition entity and compute derived active state), so creating them earlier is safe.\n\n### Test\n\nThe default `MockLCMLock.async_is_integration_connected` returns without yielding, so the existing test fixtures cannot reproduce the race. The new regression test patches it to `asyncio.sleep(0)` to mimic real provider behavior (Z-Wave JS, Matter) and asserts no `No slot coordinator` warning is logged. Verified to fail on the previous code and pass with this fix.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [x] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [ ] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR fixes or closes issue: fixes #1213\n- This PR is related to issue:\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open PR"
}
```

> AGENT

PR #1214 opened: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/pull/1214

## Summary

**Bug:** During HA startup, the warning `No slot coordinator for slot N when adding entity {in_sync,code}` fired for every slot × lock pair. Introduced in #1191 when per-lock `code`/`in_sync` entities became coordinator-dependent — but `_async_setup_new_locks` schedules those entities *before* the `slots_to_add` loop that creates the coordinators, and the per-lock `await` on `async_internal_is_integration_connected` lets the event loop drain entity-add tasks early.

**Fix:** Hoist `SlotEntityCoordinator` creation for `slots_to_add` in front of `_async_setup_new_locks`. 9-line change in `__init__.py`.

**Test:** `test_no_slot_coordinator_warning_during_initial_setup` patches the mock lock's `async_is_integration_connected` to `asyncio.sleep(0)` (mocks return synchronously, so the race wouldn't fire otherwise). Verified to fail on the unpatched code (the exact warning from the issue) and pass with the fix. All 955 tests still green.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This bug demonstrates a subtle truth about asyncio testing: mocks that return synchronously can mask races. Real provider calls (Z-Wave JS, Matter, Schlage HTTP) yield to the event loop; mocks usually don't. When a regression depends on inter-task ordering, the mock has to be made to yield (here via `asyncio.sleep(0)`) — otherwise the test passes vacuously and won't catch the regression.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```
208	        try:
209	            data = await self._lock.async_internal_get_usercodes()
210	        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
211	            self._apply_backoff()
212	            # During cold start (before the first successful poll), do not

214	            # keep coordinator-backed entities unavailable until a

218	            raise UpdateFailed from err

224	        """Perform a hard refresh to detect out-of-band code changes."""
225	        if not self.last_update_success:
226	            return
227	
228	        if self._lock_breaker.tripped:
229	            _LOGGER.debug(

231	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
232	                self._lock_breaker.failure_count,
233	            )
234	            return
235	
236	        _LOGGER.debug(

239	        )
240	        try:
241	            new_data = self._normalize_keys(
242	                await self._lock.async_internal_hard_refresh_codes()
243	            )
244	        except LockCodeManagerError as err:
245	            self._apply_backoff()
246	            _LOGGER.warning(

250	            )
251	            return
252

255	
256	        if new_data != self.data:
257	            _LOGGER.debug(
258	                "Drift detected for %s, updating coordinator data",
259	                self._lock.lock.entity_id,
260	            )
261	            self.async_set_updated_data(new_data)
```

> AGENT

## Summary

I empirically swept for hidden races by patching all five `MockLCMLock` async methods (`async_is_integration_connected`, `async_hard_refresh_codes`, `async_set_usercode`, `async_clear_usercode`, `async_get_usercodes`) to `await asyncio.sleep(0)`, then ran the full 955-test suite.

**Result:** Of 955 tests, only 3 failed — all in `test_binary_sensor.py`:
- `test_coordinator_poll_detects_external_change_and_syncs`
- `test_push_update_triggers_sync_state_change_on_binary_sensor`
- `test_drift_check_detects_external_change_and_triggers_sync`

And critically: **zero `No slot coordinator` warnings, zero `Coordinator missing` warnings, zero unhandled exceptions** appeared in the run.

### What the 3 failures actually are

All three follow the same pattern: drive an external code change → fire `async_fire_time_changed(TICK_INTERVAL * 2)` → assert `set_usercode` was called once. They fail because `async_fire_time_changed` + `async_block_till_done` doesn't drive the sync tick's chained awaits (`get_usercodes → compare → set_usercode`) to completion when each mock hop now takes an extra event-loop turn. The tick's body itself runs sequentially in production — within a single lock's tick nothing is racing with it — so adding real yields doesn't change correctness, only test pacing.

These are test-pacing brittleness, not production races. The fix (if anyone cares) would be a stronger "drive ticks to completion" helper in `conftest.py`, not changes to the integration.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Issue #1213 was uniquely surfaceable by yielding mocks because it was a true cross-task race: a *scheduled-but-not-yet-run* entity-add task on one side, and *synchronous state setup* on the other side, separated by an await. The other 3 failures don't have two parties: they're a single linear chain whose pacing is sensitive to how many event-loop turns each step takes. Different category, same symptom (the assertion fails).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### Recommendation

The race-finder I built (a 5-line `asyncio.sleep(0)` injection in `tests/common.py`) is worth keeping in mind as a debugging tool but isn't worth committing as standard test infrastructure — it would make the suite brittle to pacing without catching new categories of bugs beyond what targeted unit tests already cover.

Bottom line: **PR #1214 covers the only real production race** the empirical sweep found.

```
235	    async def async_start(self) -> None:
236	        """Start the sync manager -- discover entities, subscribe, initial tick."""
237	        if self._started:
238	            return
239	        self._started = True
240	        self._setup_state_tracking()
241	        self._setup_coordinator_listener()

247	    async def async_stop(self) -> None:
248	        """
249	        Stop the sync manager, awaiting any in-flight ticks.
250	
251	        Idempotent. Unsubscribes the timer and state listeners first so no
252	        new ticks can start, then awaits any in-flight ticks so they cannot
253	        continue to call ``_perform_sync``, ``coordinator.async_refresh``,
254	        or ``_write_state()`` after stop returns. We do not cancel them --
255	        a tick mid ``_perform_sync`` should be allowed to finish so the lock
256	        operation completes; ``_started=False`` keeps it from scheduling
257	        more work.
258	        """
259	        if not self._started:
260	            return
261	        self._started = False
262	        if self._tick_unsub:
263	            self._tick_unsub()
264	            self._tick_unsub = None
265	        self._cleanup_state_tracking()
266	        if self._coordinator_unsub:
267	            self._coordinator_unsub()
268	            self._coordinator_unsub = None
269	
270	        current = asyncio.current_task()
271	        pending = {
272	            task for task in self._tick_tasks if task is not current and not task.done()
273	        }
274	        if pending:
275	            tick_results = await asyncio.gather(*pending, return_exceptions=True)
276	            for result in tick_results:
277	                if isinstance(result, Exception) and not isinstance(
278	                    result, asyncio.CancelledError
279	                ):
280	                    _LOGGER.warning(
281	                        "%s: In-flight tick raised during stop: %s",
282	                        self._log_prefix,
283	                        result,
284	                        exc_info=result,
285	                    )
```

```
617	        """Periodic reconciliation tick."""
618	        if not self._started:
619	            return
620	
621	        # Register before the first await so a concurrent ``async_stop``
622	        # sees this tick in ``_tick_tasks``.
623	        task = asyncio.current_task()
624	        if task is None:

643	    async def _async_tick_impl(self) -> None:
644	        """
645	        Core tick logic — called from _async_tick for LOADING and OUT_OF_SYNC states.
646	
647	        This is the single authoritative place for all sync state mutations:
648	        circuit breaker tracking, sync operations, and ``_last_set_pin``
649	        changes.
650	        """
651	        # Consume any external reset requests before reading breaker state.
652	        # ``request_sync_check`` cannot reset the breaker directly because
653	        # it can fire while a tick is awaiting ``_perform_sync``, and a
654	        # mid-flight reset would clear failure state the tick is about to
655	        # read. Coalescing many requests into one reset is intentional.
656	        if self._breaker_reset_requested:
657	            self._breaker_reset_requested = False
658	            self._slot_breaker.reset()
659	
660	        slot_state = self._resolve_slot_state()
661	        if slot_state is None:
662	            # State resolution failed — stay in current state and retry.
663	            return
664	
665	        expected_in_sync = self.calculate_in_sync(slot_state)
666	
667	        # -- LOADING: detect initial sync state without performing operations --
668	        if self._state is SyncState.LOADING:
669	            if slot_state.active_state not in (STATE_ON, STATE_OFF):
670	                if not self._logged_invalid_state:
671	                    _LOGGER.debug(
672	                        "%s: Active entity has invalid state '%s', waiting "
673	                        "for valid state (ON/OFF)",
674	                        self._log_prefix,
675	                        slot_state.active_state,
676	                    )
677	                    self._logged_invalid_state = True
678	                return
679	
680	            if expected_in_sync:
681	                self._state = SyncState.IN_SYNC
682	                self._clear_resolved_issues(slot_state)
683	            else:
684	                self._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
685	
686	            _LOGGER.debug(
687	                "%s: Initial state loaded, state=%s",
688	                self._log_prefix,
689	                self._state.value,
690	            )
```

```
694	        # -- OUT_OF_SYNC: check lock reachability, then attempt sync --
695	        if self._coordinator.unreachable:
696	            self._state = SyncState.SUSPENDED
697	            self._write_state()
698	            return
699	
700	        if expected_in_sync:
701	            # Became in sync without us doing anything (external change)
702	            self._state = SyncState.IN_SYNC
703	            self._slot_breaker.reset()
704	            self._write_state()
705	            self._clear_resolved_issues(slot_state)
706	            return
707	
708	        # Circuit breaker check: too many failed sync attempts (a set that
709	        # never converges, or repeated LockOperationFailed) suspends the slot.
710	        if self._slot_breaker.tripped:
711	            _LOGGER.error(
712	                "%s: Sync attempts exceeded (%s in %s window), suspending slot",
713	                self._log_prefix,
714	                self._slot_breaker.failure_count,
715	                SYNC_ATTEMPT_WINDOW,
716	            )
717	            self._suspend_slot(
718	                slot_state,
719	                f"Lock **{self._lock.lock.entity_id}**: slot "
720	                f"**{self._slot_num}** failed to sync after "
721	                f"{self._slot_breaker.failure_count} consecutive attempts. "
722	                f"The lock may be rejecting the code silently or "
723	                f"experiencing communication issues. "
724	                f"Sync has been suspended for this slot. It will resume "
725	                f"automatically once the lock accepts the code or you change "
726	                f"the PIN for this slot.",
727	            )
728	            return
729	
730	        # Perform sync
```

> AGENT

I found the smoking gun — and it shapes what the helper needs to do.

## What's actually failing

`_execute_rate_limited` in `providers/_base.py:204-251` does `await asyncio.sleep(self._min_operation_delay - elapsed)` (real-time, 2.0s default) between successive lock operations. The rate limiter uses `time.monotonic()`, NOT `dt_util.utcnow()`, so `async_fire_time_changed` doesn't move it. When mocks yield, the tick reaches the rate limiter before `block_till_done` thinks the chain is "done" — the test returns while `set_usercode` is still suspended inside `asyncio.sleep(2)`.

Existing tests dodge this only because the sync-mock path happens to slot the operations into a different relative order with `monotonic()`.

## What the helper needs

Four jobs:
1. **Run the tick body directly** (bypass the interval timer — `async_fire_time_changed` only moves HA's mock clock).
2. **Neutralize the wall-clock rate limiter** so chained ops don't hit a real `asyncio.sleep`.
3. **Loop tick → drain → tick** until the state machine quiesces (a single tick body doesn't always complete a full set+verify cycle).
4. **Fail loudly with state info** when quiescence isn't reached, instead of letting a downstream assertion misattribute the cause.

## Sketch

```python
# tests/conftest.py
async def async_drive_sync_to(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    entity_id: str,
    target: SyncState,
    *,
    max_ticks: int = 8,
) -> None:
    """Drive a sync manager to ``target`` by repeatedly invoking ticks.

    Stronger than ``async_fire_time_changed + async_block_till_done``:
    bypasses the real-time rate limiter, calls ``_async_tick`` directly,
    and loops until the state machine reaches ``target`` (or fails fast
    with the final state if it doesn't).
    """
    entity = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, entity_id)
    sm = entity._sync_manager
    lock = sm._lock

    for step in range(max_ticks):
        if sm._state is target:
            return
        # Neutralize the wall-clock rate limiter; tests don't need it
        # and asyncio.sleep(MIN_OPERATION_DELAY) would suspend the chain
        # past async_block_till_done.
        lock._last_operation_time = 0.0
        await sm._async_tick()
        await hass.async_block_till_done()

    raise AssertionError(
        f"Sync for {entity_id} did not reach {target.value} after "
        f"{max_ticks} ticks (final: {sm._state.value}, "
        f"set_calls={len(lock.service_calls.get('set_usercode', []))})"
    )
```

The three failing tests would become:

```python
# Before:
async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + TICK_INTERVAL * 2)
await hass.async_block_till_done()
assert len(service_calls.get("set_usercode", [])) == 1

# After:
await async_drive_sync_to(hass, in_sync_entity, SyncState.IN_SYNC)
assert len(service_calls["set_usercode"]) == 1
```

## What this trades away

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
This helper hides three real production behaviors: (1) the wall-clock rate limiter, (2) timer-driven scheduling, (3) the multi-tick handoff between OUT_OF_SYNC → SYNCING → verify. If you specifically want to test those, you don't want this helper — you want targeted unit tests of each. The helper is right for tests whose intent is "did the integration ultimately do the right thing?" not "did it do it via the right path?"
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

A more defensible variant injects a context-manager fixture that **lowers `MIN_OPERATION_DELAY` to 0 for the scope of the test** (one line, no helper needed for most cases), then keeps the existing `async_fire_time_changed` flow:

```python
@pytest.fixture
def no_rate_limit():
    with patch.object(BaseLock, "_min_operation_delay", 0.0):
        yield
```

That's the minimum intervention — it doesn't change *what* the test exercises, only removes the real-time wait. It would unblock the 3 failures without hiding any state-machine behavior. The bigger helper is only needed when you genuinely want "drive to quiescence regardless of how many ticks it takes."

## Recommendation

I'd land *both* in a test-infra PR, keep the existing tests on `async_fire_time_changed`, add `no_rate_limit` as autouse for tests that exercise lock operations, and reserve `async_drive_sync_to` for tests where ticks naturally chain (drift check, push update correction). But this is genuinely separate from #1213 — want me to open it as its own PR, or is this just the design sketch you wanted?

> DEVELOPER

yes open as its own PR

Pound block_till_done until set_usercode appears or we give up\n    for i in range(20):\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n        n = len(lock.service_calls.get(\"set_usercode\", []))\n        if n >= 1:\n            print(f\"set_usercode landed after {i+1} block_till_done iterations\")\n            break\n    else:\n        print(f\"set_usercode never landed after 20 block_till_done iterations\")\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(entry.entry_id)\nEOF\ncp /tmp/test_diag.py tests/test_diag.py\nuv run pytest tests/test_diag.py -v -s 2>&1 | grep -E \"^set_usercode|PASSED|FAILED|never landed|iterations\" | head -5",

> AGENT

**Root cause confirmed**: `block_till_done` returns once before the chain completes; a second call lands the work. The fix is a polling helper that calls `block_till_done` repeatedly until a stable condition is met.

Let me clean up and build the helper:

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Found the exact mechanism: `_TrackTimeInterval._run_action` (helpers/event.py:1643) runs the tick with `background=True`. Background tasks are NOT awaited by `block_till_done` unless you pass `wait_background_tasks=True`. With sync mocks the background tick completes synchronously enough to slip in under the wire; with yielding mocks it doesn't.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me verify before building the helper:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_012y7CPVstaBe43kVXHJMPJ4
```json
{
  "command": "cat > tests/test_verify_bg.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Verify: passing wait_background_tasks=True alone fixes the failing tests.\"\"\"\nimport asyncio\nfrom homeassistant.core import HomeAssistant\nfrom homeassistant.const import CONF_NAME, CONF_PIN, CONF_ENABLED\nfrom homeassistant.util import dt as dt_util\nfrom pytest_homeassistant_custom_component.common import MockConfigEntry, async_fire_time_changed\nfrom custom_components.lock_code_manager.const import DOMAIN, CONF_LOCKS, CONF_SLOTS\nfrom custom_components.lock_code_manager.domain.sync import TICK_INTERVAL\nfrom tests.common import LOCK_1_ENTITY_ID, MockLCMLock\nfrom tests.conftest import async_initial_tick\n\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\nasync def test_bg_wait(hass: HomeAssistant, mock_lock_config_entry):\n    async def yielding(self):\n        await asyncio.sleep(0)\n        return self._connected\n    config = {CONF_LOCKS: [LOCK_1_ENTITY_ID], CONF_SLOTS: {1: {CONF_NAME: \"t1\", CONF_PIN: \"1234\", CONF_ENABLED: True}}}\n    entry = MockConfigEntry(domain=DOMAIN, data=config, unique_id=\"BG\", title=\"BG\")\n    entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n    with patch.object(MockLCMLock, \"async_is_integration_connected\", yielding):\n        await hass.config_entries.async_setup(entry.entry_id)\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n        await async_initial_tick(hass, \"binary_sensor.test_1_code_slot_1_in_sync\")\n        lock = entry.runtime_data.locks[LOCK_1_ENTITY_ID]\n        lock.service_calls.get(\"set_usercode\", []).clear()\n        lock.codes[1] = \"9999\"\n        await lock.coordinator.async_refresh()\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n        async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + TICK_INTERVAL * 2)\n        # Single block_till_done WITH wait_background_tasks=True\n        await hass.async_block_till_done(wait_background_tasks=True)\n        n = len(lock.service_calls.get(\"set_usercode\", []))\n        print(f\"set_usercode calls: {n}\")\n        assert n == 1\n        await hass.config_entries.async_unload(entry.entry_id)\nEOF\nuv run pytest tests/test_verify_bg.py -v -s 2>&1 | grep -E \"PASSED|FAILED|set_usercode calls|AssertionError\" | head -5",
```

```
201	def get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass: HomeAssistant, entity_id: str):
202	    """
203	    Get the in-sync entity object for a given entity ID.
204	
205	    Returns the entity object from the entity component registry.
206	    """
207	    entity_component = hass.data["entity_components"]["binary_sensor"]
208	    entity_obj = entity_component.get_entity(entity_id)
209	    assert entity_obj is not None
210	    return entity_obj
211	
212	
213	async def async_trigger_sync_tick(
214	    hass: HomeAssistant, entity_id: str, set_dirty: bool = True
215	) -> None:
216	    """
217	    Manually trigger a sync tick for an in-sync entity.
218	
219	    Encapsulates the pattern of forcing an entity into a tickable state and
220	    triggering an immediate tick, useful for testing tick-based sync behavior
221	    without waiting for the natural 5-second tick interval.
222	    """
223	    entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, entity_id)
224	    if set_dirty and entity_obj._sync_manager._state not in (
225	        SyncState.LOADING,
226	        SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC,
227	    ):
228	        entity_obj._sync_manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
229	    await entity_obj._sync_manager._async_tick()
230	    await hass.async_block_till_done()
231	
232	
233	async def async_initial_tick(hass: HomeAssistant, entity_id: str) -> None:
234	    """
235	    Trigger initial tick for entity setup.
236	
237	    During entity setup, the initial tick in async_start may fail if dependent
238	    entities (active, code sensor) are not yet registered. This helper triggers
239	    a tick to complete initial state loading, but only if the entity hasn't
240	    been initialized yet (state is LOADING).
241	    """
242	    entity_obj = get_in_sync_entity_obj(hass, entity_id)
243	    if entity_obj._sync_manager._state is SyncState.LOADING:
244	        await entity_obj._sync_manager._async_tick()
245	        await hass.async_block_till_done()
246	
247	
248	async def async_trigger_sync_tick_for_manager(
249	    hass: HomeAssistant, sync_manager, set_dirty: bool = True
250	) -> None:
251	    """
252	    Manually trigger a sync tick for a sync manager object.
253	
254	    Useful when you already have the entity object or need to trigger
255	    ticks on multiple managers in a loop.
256	    """
257	    if set_dirty and sync_manager._state not in (
258	        SyncState.LOADING,
259	        SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC,
260	    ):
261	        sync_manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
262	    await sync_manager._async_tick()
263	    await hass.async_block_till_done()
264
```

```json
  "new_string": "async def async_trigger_sync_tick_for_manager(\n    hass: HomeAssistant, sync_manager, set_dirty: bool = True\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"\n    Manually trigger a sync tick for a sync manager object.\n\n    Useful when you already have the entity object or need to trigger\n    ticks on multiple managers in a loop.\n    \"\"\"\n    if set_dirty and sync_manager._state not in (\n        SyncState.LOADING,\n        SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC,\n    ):\n        sync_manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC\n    await sync_manager._async_tick()\n    await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n\nasync def async_advance_time(hass: HomeAssistant, delta: timedelta) -> None:\n    \"\"\"\n    Advance HA's mock clock by ``delta`` and settle pending work.\n\n    Replaces the brittle ``async_fire_time_changed + async_block_till_done``\n    pair. ``async_track_time_interval`` (used for the sync tick and the\n    coordinator update interval) schedules its callback as a background\n    task via ``async_run_hass_job(..., background=True)``. The default\n    ``async_block_till_done`` only waits for foreground tasks, so chains\n    spawned by a timer-fired tick can outlive the wait and leak across\n    assertions. This helper passes ``wait_background_tasks=True`` to\n    cover them.\n    \"\"\"\n    async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + delta)\n    await hass.async_block_till_done(wait_background_tasks=True)"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/test_binary_sensor.py",
  "old_string": "    # Verify back in sync\n    await async_advance_time(hass, TICK_INTERVAL * 3)\n\n    state = hass.states.get(in_sync_entity)\n    assert state.state == STATE_ON, \"Should be in sync after drift correction\"\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(config_entry.entry_id)\n\n\nasync def test_sync_manager_handles_code_sensor_unknown_state_on_startup(",
  "new_string": "    # Verify back in sync\n    await async_advance_time(hass, TICK_INTERVAL * 3)\n\n    state = hass.states.get(in_sync_entity)\n    assert state.state == STATE_ON, \"Should be in sync after drift correction\"\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(config_entry.entry_id)\n\n\nasync def test_async_advance_time_drains_background_tick_chain(\n    hass: HomeAssistant,\n    mock_lock_config_entry,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"``async_advance_time`` settles a background tick whose mocks yield.\n\n    ``async_track_time_interval`` (used by the sync tick and coordinator\n    update) runs its callback as a background task. The bare\n    ``async_fire_time_changed + async_block_till_done`` pair does not\n    wait for background tasks, so any tick whose chain spans more than\n    one event-loop turn can outlive the wait. This is masked today only\n    because the default mock returns synchronously; the moment a mock\n    or a real provider yields, the bare pattern stops settling. This\n    test forces the issue by patching the connection check to yield,\n    and asserts ``async_advance_time`` still settles correctly.\n    \"\"\"\n    config = {\n        CONF_LOCKS: [LOCK_1_ENTITY_ID],\n        CONF_SLOTS: {1: {CONF_NAME: \"t1\", CONF_PIN: \"1234\", CONF_ENABLED: True}},\n    }\n    config_entry = MockConfigEntry(\n        domain=DOMAIN, data=config, unique_id=\"advance_time_test\", title=\"Test\"\n    )\n    config_entry.add_to_hass(hass)\n\n    async def _yielding_connected(self: MockLCMLock) -> bool:\n        await asyncio.sleep(0)\n        return self._connected\n\n    with patch.object(\n        MockLCMLock, \"async_is_integration_connected\", _yielding_connected\n    ):\n        await hass.config_entries.async_setup(config_entry.entry_id)\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n        in_sync_entity = \"binary_sensor.test_1_code_slot_1_in_sync\"\n        await async_initial_tick(hass, in_sync_entity)\n\n        lock_provider = config_entry.runtime_data.locks[LOCK_1_ENTITY_ID]\n        lock_provider.service_calls.get(\"set_usercode\", []).clear()\n        lock_provider.codes[1] = \"9999\"\n        await lock_provider.coordinator.async_refresh()\n        await hass.async_block_till_done()\n\n        await async_advance_time(hass, TICK_INTERVAL * 2)\n\n        assert len(lock_provider.service_calls[\"set_usercode\"]) == 1\n\n    await hass.config_entries.async_unload(config_entry.entry_id)\n\n\nasync def test_sync_manager_handles_code_sensor_unknown_state_on_startup("
}
```

```
     MockConfigEntry,
     MockModule,
     MockPlatform,
+    async_fire_time_changed,
     mock_config_flow,
     mock_integration,
     mock_platform,
@@ -24,6 +26,7 @@ from homeassistant.const import Platform
 from homeassistant.core import HomeAssistant
 from homeassistant.helpers.entity_platform import AddEntitiesCallback

+from homeassistant.util import dt as dt_util

@@ -261,3 +264,20 @@ async def async_trigger_sync_tick_for_manager(
         sync_manager._state = SyncState.OUT_OF_SYNC
     await sync_manager._async_tick()
     await hass.async_block_till_done()
+
+
+async def async_advance_time(hass: HomeAssistant, delta: timedelta) -> None:
+    """
+    Advance HA's mock clock by ``delta`` and settle pending work.
+
+    Replaces the brittle ``async_fire_time_changed + async_block_till_done``
+    pair. ``async_track_time_interval`` (used for the sync tick and the
+    coordinator update interval) schedules its callback as a background
+    task via ``async_run_hass_job(..., background=True)``. The default
+    ``async_block_till_done`` only waits for foreground tasks, so chains
+    spawned by a timer-fired tick can outlive the wait and leak across
+    assertions. This helper passes ``wait_background_tasks=True`` to
+    cover them.
+    """
+    async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + delta)
+    await hass.async_block_till_done(wait_background_tasks=True)
```

The default\n``async_block_till_done`` only waits for foreground tasks, so a\nchain spawned by a timer-fired tick can outlive the wait and leak\nacross assertions. Today's tests pass only because the synchronous\nmock methods complete in fewer event-loop turns than the wait\nallows -- any yielding mock or real provider call (Z-Wave JS,\nMatter, Schlage) exposes the race and the bare pattern stops\nsettling reliably.\n\nThe helper wraps ``async_fire_time_changed + async_block_till_done\n(wait_background_tasks=True)`` and is what tests should reach for\nwhenever they fire a time-based scheduler. Refactor the 9 existing\ncall sites in test_binary_sensor.py to use it.\n\nAdd a regression test that patches three mock lock methods to yield\non entry, triggers a tick-fired sync, and asserts the helper still\nsettles. Verified to fail when the helper drops the\n``wait_background_tasks=True`` flag.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\"",

id: toolu_01EaGYafA2goPzDA1CCBs6mk
```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --title \"test: add async_advance_time helper for background-task tick chains\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Proposed change\n\nThe bare `async_fire_time_changed(...) + async_block_till_done()` pair that the binary-sensor tests use to drive timer-fired ticks is brittle. `async_track_time_interval` (which schedules both the sync tick and the coordinator update interval) runs its callback via `hass.async_run_hass_job(..., background=True)` — so the tick task is a **background** task. The default `async_block_till_done` only awaits foreground tasks (`wait_background_tasks=False`); it returns before the tick's chained awaits complete. The existing 3 \\\"external change → tick → sync\\\" tests in `test_binary_sensor.py` pass today only because the synchronous mock methods complete in fewer event-loop turns than the bare wait happens to allow. The moment any mock or real provider call (Z-Wave JS, Matter, Schlage HTTP) genuinely yields, those tests stop settling reliably.\n\nThis PR adds a small helper that wraps the correct call:\n\n```python\nasync def async_advance_time(hass: HomeAssistant, delta: timedelta) -> None:\n    async_fire_time_changed(hass, dt_util.utcnow() + delta)\n    await hass.async_block_till_done(wait_background_tasks=True)\n```\n\n…and refactors the 9 existing usage sites in `test_binary_sensor.py` to use it. Net diff is small and behavior-preserving for the existing suite.\n\n### Regression test\n\n`test_async_advance_time_drains_background_tick_chain` patches three `MockLCMLock` methods (`async_set_usercode`, `async_get_usercodes`, `async_is_integration_connected`) to `await asyncio.sleep(0)` on entry — enough event-loop turns to expose the race — and asserts that `async_advance_time` still settles. Verified to fail when the helper drops `wait_background_tasks=True`.\n\n### Why this matters\n\nDiscovered while empirically probing for hidden races (a follow-up from #1214). Yielding mocks broke 3 tests; the diagnosis tracked back to this helper-shape gap, not to a real production bug. Documenting the gotcha in one place and providing the right shape prevents future tests from rediscovering it.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [x] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR fixes or closes issue:\n- This PR is related to issue: #1213, #1214\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Open PR"
}
```

> AGENT

PR #1215 opened: https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/pull/1215

## Summary

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The actual mechanism turned out to be exactly one line in HA's `helpers/event.py:1643` — `_TrackTimeInterval` schedules its tick callback with `background=True`. That single keyword flips the task into a category that `async_block_till_done` ignores by default. My earlier hypothesis about the rate limiter was wrong (already disabled by autouse fixture). The right fix isn't a polling loop, it's a one-arg flip: `wait_background_tasks=True`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

What landed:

- **`async_advance_time(hass, delta)`** in `tests/conftest.py` — wraps `async_fire_time_changed + async_block_till_done(wait_background_tasks=True)`. 5 lines of code, 10 lines of docstring explaining the gotcha.
- **9 call-site refactors** in `test_binary_sensor.py` — including the `_async_force_sync_cycle` helper. Net diff is behavior-preserving for the existing suite.
- **Regression test** `test_async_advance_time_drains_background_tick_chain` — patches three mock methods to yield, fires a timer-driven tick, asserts the helper still settles. Verified to fail when `wait_background_tasks=True` is dropped from the helper.

All 955 tests pass.

Independent of #1214; can land in either order.

> AGENT

Yes — `custom_components/lock_code_manager/icons.json` exists (34 lines).

It defines entity-key icons for:
- `binary_sensor.in_sync` — default `mdi:sync-alert`, on=`mdi:sync`, off=`mdi:sync-off`
- `event.pin_used` — `mdi:gesture-tap`
- `sensor.code` — `mdi:lock-smart`
- `text.name` — `mdi:rename`
- `text.pin` — `mdi:form-textbox-password`

And one service icon: `hard_refresh_usercodes` → `mdi:refresh`.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
HA reads this file via the `translation_key` on each entity (or service name). Notably absent here: the `switch` platform (the slot Enabled switch), and the `active` binary sensor — they fall back to HA's default platform icons. If you wanted, e.g., a custom icon for `binary_sensor.active`, you'd add an `"active"` key under `binary_sensor`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
HA's `icons.json` keys entities by `translation_key` and services by service name. The `binary_sensor.in_sync` entry uses a `"state"` map so the icon flips between on/off — useful for any binary state where you want a visual cue. The `binary_sensor.active` entry would benefit from the same pattern (active vs. inactive should look different at a glance).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Here's what's missing:

| Type | Key | Meaning |
|------|-----|---------|
| binary_sensor | `active` | Slot's PIN is currently active per its schedule/condition |
| switch | `enabled` | Slot enabled toggle |
| service | `set_usercode` | Manually set a usercode on a lock |
| service | `clear_usercode` | Manually clear a usercode |
| service | `set_slot_condition` | Assign a condition entity to a slot |
| service | `clear_slot_condition` | Remove a slot's condition entity |
| service | `generate_pin` | Generate a random PIN |

The entity icons matter most for users (visible in the dashboard); service icons matter less (only seen in dev tools). Want input on the two entity icons since they shape what dashboards look like:

```
1	"""Utility functions for lock_code_manager."""
2	
3	from __future__ import annotations
4	
5	import logging
6	import zlib
7	
8	from homeassistant.components.switch import DOMAIN as SWITCH_DOMAIN, SERVICE_TURN_OFF
9	from homeassistant.const import ATTR_ENTITY_ID, CONF_ENABLED
10	from homeassistant.core import HomeAssistant
11	from homeassistant.helpers import entity_registry as er
12	from homeassistant.helpers.issue_registry import IssueSeverity, async_create_issue
13	
14	from ..const import DOMAIN
15	from .config import build_slot_unique_id
16	
17	_LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__)
18	
19	
20	def mask_pin(
21	    pin: str | None,
22	    slot_num: int | str,
23	    instance_id: str,
24	) -> str:
25	    """
26	    Return a deterministic masked representation of a PIN for logging.
27	
28	    Uses CRC32 salted with the HA instance ID and slot number so the same
29	    PIN on the same slot always produces the same 8-char hex token regardless
30	    of which lock or config entry it belongs to.  Different slots or HA
31	    instances produce different tokens.
32	    """
33	    if not pin:
34	        return "<empty>"
35	    salt = f"{instance_id}:{slot_num}:{pin}"
36	    return f"pin#{zlib.crc32(salt.encode()) & 0xFFFFFFFF:08x}"
37	
38	
39	async def async_disable_slot(
40	    hass: HomeAssistant,
41	    ent_reg: er.EntityRegistry,
42	    config_entry_id: str,
43	    slot_num: int,
44	    *,
45	    reason: str | None = None,
46	    lock_name: str | None = None,
47	    lock_entity_id: str | None = None,
48	) -> bool:
49	    """
50	    Disable a slot via the enabled switch and optionally create a repair issue.
51	
52	    Returns True if the switch was found and turned off, False otherwise.
53	    When reason is provided, a repair issue is created so the user can
54	    acknowledge it through the Home Assistant repairs dashboard.
55	    """
56	    enabled_entity_id = ent_reg.async_get_entity_id(
57	        SWITCH_DOMAIN,
58	        DOMAIN,
59	        build_slot_unique_id(config_entry_id, slot_num, CONF_ENABLED),
60	    )
61	    if not enabled_entity_id:
62	        lock_context = f" on {lock_name} ({lock_entity_id})" if lock_name else ""
63	        _LOGGER.warning(
64	            "Cannot disable slot %s%s — switch entity not found",
65	            slot_num,
66	            lock_context,
67	        )
68	        return False
69	
70	    await hass.services.async_call(
71	        SWITCH_DOMAIN,
72	        SERVICE_TURN_OFF,
73	        {ATTR_ENTITY_ID: enabled_entity_id},
74	        blocking=True,
75	    )
76	
77	    if reason:
78	        issue_id = f"slot_disabled_{config_entry_id}_{slot_num}"
79	        async_create_issue(
80	            hass,
81	            DOMAIN,
82	            issue_id,
83	            is_fixable=True,
84	            is_persistent=True,
85	            severity=IssueSeverity.WARNING,
86	            translation_key="slot_disabled",
87	            translation_placeholders={
88	                "slot_num": str(slot_num),
89	                "reason": reason,
90	            },
91	        )
92	
93	    return True
94
```

> AGENT

Now I understand the mechanism. `mask_pin(pin, slot_num, instance_id)` → `pin#xxxxxxxx` is CRC32 of `f"{instance_id}:{slot_num}:{pin}"` — deterministic and per-instance. To reverse it, we enumerate every currently-configured `(slot_num, pin)` pair across all loaded LCM entries, precompute their masked tokens, and substitute matches in the input text.

## Design proposal

**Service**: `lock_code_manager.deobfuscate_log`
- **Input**: `log_text` (string) — pasted log content
- **Output (service response)**:
  - `deobfuscated_text`: input with `pin#xxxxxxxx` tokens replaced by real PINs
  - `summary`: `{matched: int, unmatched_tokens: list[str], total: int}`

**Lookup**: aggregate `(slot_num, pin)` from every LCM entry's config + the instance_id from `hass.data[DOMAIN]["instance_id"]`. Build a `{token: pin}` dict once per call.

**Privacy floor**:
- Service response only — never logged, never notification.
- `services.yaml` description includes "**Warning**: response contains plaintext PINs. Don't share."
- Wrap the deobfuscated text with sentinel markers like `=== BEGIN DEOBFUSCATED — DO NOT SHARE ===` and `=== END ===` so users know to redact before pasting anywhere.

## Tradeoffs to weigh

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The mask uses **CRC32** (not a cryptographic hash) — it's not designed to resist an attacker who knows the algorithm and salt. The existing masking is best understood as *log redaction for casual sharing*, not as security. This deobfuscation service is exactly the inverse — and that's fine. A motivated attacker with the instance_id and salt format could already brute-force a 4–8 digit PIN against a CRC32 token in milliseconds. The service doesn't change the threat model; it just makes the user's legitimate debugging easier.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Two real design questions I'd like input on:

```
9	
10	from ..const import EXCLUDED_CONDITION_PLATFORMS
11	from .locks import get_managed_lock

13	
14	
15	async def async_set_usercode(
16	    hass: HomeAssistant, lock_entity_id: str, code_slot: int, usercode: str
17	) -> None:
18	    """Set a usercode on a lock slot."""
19	    usercode = usercode.strip()
20	    if not usercode:
21	        raise ServiceValidationError(
22	            "Usercode must not be empty; use the clear operation instead"
23	        )
24	    lock = get_managed_lock(hass, lock_entity_id)
25	    await lock.async_internal_set_usercode(code_slot, usercode)
26	
27	
28	async def async_clear_usercode(
29	    hass: HomeAssistant, lock_entity_id: str, code_slot: int
30	) -> None:
31	    """Clear a usercode from a lock slot."""
32	    lock = get_managed_lock(hass, lock_entity_id)
33	    await lock.async_internal_clear_usercode(code_slot)
34	
35	
36	async def async_set_slot_condition(
37	    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry_id: str, slot: int, entity_id: str
38	) -> None:
39	    """Set a condition entity for a slot."""
40	    config_entry = get_loaded_config_entry(hass, config_entry_id)
41	    config = get_entry_config(config_entry)
42	    if not config.has_slot(slot):
43	        raise ServiceValidationError(f"Slot {slot} not found in config entry")
44	
45	    if not hass.states.get(entity_id):
46	        raise ServiceValidationError(f"Entity {entity_id} not found")
47	
48	    # Check for excluded platforms
49	    ent_reg = er.async_get(hass)
50	    entity_entry = ent_reg.async_get(entity_id)
51	    if entity_entry and entity_entry.platform in EXCLUDED_CONDITION_PLATFORMS:
52	        raise ServiceValidationError(
53	            f"Entities from the '{entity_entry.platform}' integration are not "
54	            "supported as condition entities. See the wiki for details: "
55	            "https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/wiki/"
56	            "Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations"
57	        )
58	
59	    new_config = config.with_slot_field_set(slot, CONF_ENTITY_ID, entity_id)
60	    hass.config_entries.async_update_entry(config_entry, options=new_config.to_dict())
61	
62	
63	async def async_clear_slot_condition(
64	    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry_id: str, slot: int
65	) -> None:
66	    """Clear the condition entity from a slot."""
67	    config_entry = get_loaded_config_entry(hass, config_entry_id)
68	    config = get_entry_config(config_entry)
69	    if not config.has_slot(slot):
70	        raise ServiceValidationError(f"Slot {slot} not found in config entry")
71	
72	    new_config = config.with_slot_field_removed(slot, CONF_ENTITY_ID)
73	    hass.config_entries.async_update_entry(config_entry, options=new_config.to_dict())
74
```
